```json
    "content": "docs/paper/related_work.tex:5:\\citet{shumailov2024_nature} demonstrate that recursive training on model outputs erodes distributional tails; \\citet{dohmatob2024_tale_of_tails} formalize this via scaling laws \\cite{seddik2024synthetic}, \\citet{dohmatob2025_strong_collapse} establish strong collapse guarantees, and the MAD framework \\cite{alemohammad2024_mad} extends the analysis to self-consuming models.\ndocs/paper/related_work.tex:6:Subsequent work addresses mitigation \\cite{gerstgrasser2024_accumulate,drayson2025_detection_prevents_collapse,yi2026_escaping_collapse,falahati2026_curated,wenzek2020_ccnet}, non-monotonic dynamics \\cite{bakshi2026_collapse_improvement}, convergence \\cite{marchi2024_heat_death,wang2026_contamination,knowledge_collapse2025,dohmatob2024_demystified}, and diversity decline \\cite{guo2024curious,briesch2024selfconsuming,padmakumar2024diversity,sigma2026_spectral,synthetic_eggs2026}.\ndocs/paper/related_work.tex:7:\\citet{schaeffer2025} argue that ``model collapse'' conflates multiple distinct phenomena, and call for operationally precise metrics.\ndocs/paper/related_work.tex:8:Dohmatob et al.\\ predict that recursive training erodes distributional tails as a theoretical scaling law \\citep{dohmatob2024_tale_of_tails}; we operationalize this prediction into a deployable ordinal classifier, revealing strategy-dependent trajectories and non-monotonic 7B behavior that the theory does not predict.\ndocs/paper/related_work.tex:12:[Omitted long matching line]\ndocs/paper/related_work.tex:13:M4-SemEval \\cite{wang2024_m4_semeval}, RAID \\cite{dugan2024_raid}, and MAGE \\cite{li2024_mage} benchmark detection across domains, models, and adversarial conditions.\ndocs/paper/related_work.tex:17:EditLens \\cite{editlens2026} estimates editing extent but does not model recursive chains; \\citet{uchendu2020_attribution} classify \\emph{which} model generated text; LOKI \\cite{loki2025} benchmarks synthetic data detection across modalities.\ndocs/paper/related_work.tex:18:\\citet{mohamed2025_broken_telephone} study iterative generation as a ``broken telephone'' effect, characterizing \\emph{how} text degrades without classifying which iteration a passage comes from.\ndocs/paper/related_work.tex:22:Ordinal classification has a long history \\cite{mccullagh1980_ordinal}: \\citet{frank2001} reduce ordinal problems to nested binary tasks, and \\citet{rennie2005} introduce loss functions tailored to discrete ordered labels.\ndocs/paper/related_work.tex:23:CORAL \\cite{coral2020} enforces rank consistency via ordered binary tasks; CLOC \\cite{cloc2025} extends contrastive learning with multi-margin ordinal losses.\ndocs/paper/related_work.tex:27:Decoding strategies shape text statistics in ways that interact with depth signals \\cite{holtzman2020_nucleus,finlayson2024_closing,garces_arias2025_decoding_decoded,wiher2022_decoding,basu2021_mirostat}.\ndocs/paper/atlas.tex:62:This transition aligns with model-collapse dynamics~\\cite{shumailov2024_nature}: early recursion narrows the surprisal distribution (captured by its variance), while later steps erode the perplexity tail (captured by upper quantiles and kurtosis).\ndocs/paper/atlas.tex:170:Temperature ($\\tau{=}0.9$) yields the highest accuracy in 2/3 models (GPT-2~XL 85.9\\%, OLMo 82.1\\%) because uniform logit scaling accelerates convergence to an attractor distribution~\\cite{shumailov2024_nature}, amplifying the statistical fingerprint. However, this acceleration creates a boundary reversal risk: temperature depth-$N$ features approximate nucleus/top-$k$ depth-$(N{+}1)$ features, causing cross-strategy classifiers to systematically overpredict depth (\\S\\ref{sec:generalization}).\ndocs/paper/acl.sty:190:\\renewcommand\\cite{\\citep}  % to get \"(Author Year)\" with natbib\ndocs/paper/conclusion.tex:30:Adversarial paraphrasing \\cite{sadasivan2023_paraphrase} could disrupt the perplexity tail features that drive classification; depth estimation's robustness under such attacks is untested.\ndocs/paper/conclusion.tex:34:However, \\citet{bakshi2026_collapse_improvement} show that early recursion can temporarily \\emph{improve} model quality, which could produce non-monotonic AUC profiles (e.g., a dip at d1/d2 followed by recovery at d2/d3).\ndocs/paper/conclusion.tex:55:Detection features degrade under domain shift \\cite{pudasaini2026_detection_fails}, and curated synthetic data may not trigger collapse \\cite{falahati2026_curated}, attenuating the fingerprints we exploit.\ndocs/paper/discussion.tex:5:LoRA fine-tuning loss decreases monotonically with depth for all three families, a direct empirical validation of the tail erosion predicted by \\citet{dohmatob2024_tale_of_tails}; instruction tuning regularizes this compression (\\S\\ref{sec:gen:crossscale}).\ndocs/paper/acl_natbib.bst:55: %   \\citet{key} ==>>                Jones et al. 665	Depth estimation characterizes \emph{iterative reuse} regardless of generator cooperation or identity.
```
```text
666	A layered provenance system combining these three signals (watermark presence, source model, and recursive generation depth) could support more nuanced data auditing than any single method.
667	
668	\subsection{Computational Resources and Reproducibility}
669	\label{app:compute}
670	
671	\paragraph{Hardware.}
672	All experiments were conducted on 2$\times$ NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 (48\,GB) GPUs locally and 2$\times$ NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 (48\,GB) on cloud instances.
673	Total computation is approximately 200 GPU$\cdot$hours, covering the $3 \times 3$ generation matrix (LoRA fine-tuning + sampling at each depth), feature extraction, depth extension to d5, 7--8B QLoRA experiments, and all ablations.
674	
675	\paragraph{Software.}
676	Key library versions: PyTorch 2.x, Transformers 4.x, PEFT 0.x, scikit-learn 1.x.
677	Exact versions will be pinned in the released code repository.
678	
679	\paragraph{Hyperparameters.}
680	LoRA hyperparameters ($r{=}16$, $\alpha{=}32$, dropout $0.05$, lr $2{\times}10^{-4}$, batch size 8, 3 epochs) follow \citet{hu2022lora}.
681	The Random Forest classifier uses scikit-learn defaults (100 trees, Gini criterion, no max depth) without hyperparameter search; all reported results use 5-fold stratified cross-validation.
682	
683	\paragraph{Inference cost.}
684	Feature extraction and classification require no GPU.
685	Inference takes approximately 2 minutes per 5{,}000 samples on a single CPU core (reference-model forward pass + RF prediction), enabling deployment without specialized hardware.
686	
```
